        Idea:
# Sprint 0B: Thresholds and Golden Corpus

## Overall Context

This follows Sprint 0A's contract freeze and creates the comparison oracles consumed by every later parity, harness, and canary sprint. The epic cannot rely on subjective media review; later sprints need route-keyed baselines and a single threshold source of truth.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Preserve queue contracts, output shapes, memory-profile behavior, latency expectations, and rollback.
- Use route keys, not friendly names, for baselines and cutover evidence.
- Keep active non-RayWorker routes covered as preservation fixtures even when they stay outside RayWorker.
- Keep `docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md` synchronized with threshold and route versions.

## Sprint Goal

Produce executable comparison oracles and WGP golden corpora for later migration sprints.

## Required Deliverables

- `migration-thresholds.yaml`.
- WGP self-repeatability report.
- Route-keyed WGP golden corpus for Cohort A/B and representative Cohort E routes.
- Lightweight product-contract fixtures for `video_enhance`, `image-upscale`, `animate_character`, and `flux_klein_edit`.
- Live-validation document updated to the same route/threshold version.

## Exit Criteria

Threshold YAML is committed and read by a smoke script; WGP self-drift is below thresholds or affected routes are marked WGP-only/pending; corpus is route-keyed; non-RayWorker active routes have owner-approved fixtures or explicit deferral rationale.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 0B — Thresholds and Golden Corpus

## Overview

Sprint 0A froze the project generation contract; the executor produced `reigh-worker/docs/migration-baselines.md` with USED inventories and per-task contract skeletons, but did not produce any executable comparison artifacts. Sprint 0B turns §11 of `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md` from a written table into a committed YAML that downstream scripts read, plus a route-keyed corpus and product fixtures so later sprints (3 dual-run, 3.5 dry run, 11 canary) all reference one source of truth. Live WGP self-repeatability needs RunPod GPU time and is constrained by the Sprint 0C 50→200 GB disk bump; the plan lands the framework first and gates live calibration on cohort scope.

Key constraints:
- `reigh-worker/` is a nested git repo (Sprint 0A retro #1/#2). All artifacts under `reigh-worker/` must be committed in that repo, not the workspace top level.
- §11 fixes the artifact home: `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/migration-thresholds.yaml` and `…/golden/<route_key>/`. Neither path exists yet.
- Route-key composition is mandated (§11): Cohort E uses `(task_type, model_name/model_family, guidance_kind, continuity_case, profile)`; Cohort B uses edit-model variant + mask/annotation/style-reference + profile.
- Memory note: WAN 14B alone fills the current 50 GB pod disk; Cohort E live drift runs must wait on Sprint 0C unless framed as pending.

## Phase 1: Threshold Artifact and Smoke Reader

### Step 1: Survey current consumers of §11 thresholds (`docs/migration-vibecomfy.md`, `reigh-worker/scripts/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirm** the §11 table values at `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md:1383` are the source of truth and have not drifted.
2. **Confirm** there is no existing `dual_run_compare/` tree under `reigh-worker/scripts/` that we'd overwrite (already verified: directory is absent).

### Step 2: Author `migration-thresholds.yaml` (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/migration-thresholds.yaml`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the file with three top-level sections:
   - `version: "0B-2026-05-05"` (read by Sprint 3/3.5/11 to detect schema drift).
   - `defaults:` mirroring the §11 table verbatim (image dual-run, video dual-run, latency, VRAM, OOM, canary divergence) with each metric carrying `metric`, `threshold`, `failure_action`.
   - `routes:` keyed by `route_key` (see Phase 2) with optional `override` blocks and a required `calibration_status` field (`green | pending_calibration | wgp_only | deferred`) plus `notes`.
2. **Seed** every Cohort A/B route_key plus the representative Cohort E rows with `calibration_status: pending_calibration` and a one-line rationale; routes blocked by the Sprint 0C disk bump get `notes: "blocked: pod disk 50 GB; revisit after Sprint 0C 200 GB"`.

### Step 3: Add a thresholds parser/smoke script (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/__init__.py`, `thresholds.py`, `check_thresholds.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `thresholds.py:load_thresholds(path) -> Thresholds` returning a typed dataclass with `defaults`, `routes`, `version` and a `for_route(route_key)` accessor that merges defaults + override.
2. **Implement** `check_thresholds.py` as a CLI: `python -m scripts.dual_run_compare.check_thresholds [--strict]` — loads YAML, validates required §11 metric keys are present, prints route_key + calibration_status table, exits non-zero on schema gaps.
3. **Add** unit tests at `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_thresholds.py` covering: file loads, every §11 metric present in `defaults`, override merge picks the route value over the default, schema check fails when a metric is missing.

## Phase 2: Route-Key Schema and Golden Corpus Layout

### Step 4: Define route-key derivation (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/route_keys.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `derive_route_key(task_type, model_name=None, guidance_kind=None, continuity_case=None, profile=None) -> str`. Serialization: lowercase slug `<task_type>__<model_family>__<guidance>__<continuity>__profile<N>`, with `na` fillers for cohorts where a field doesn't apply (image cohorts collapse `guidance_kind` and `continuity_case` to `na`).
2. **Map** `model_name → model_family` for the cases listed in `reigh-worker/scripts/worker_matrix_cases.json` and `migration-vibecomfy.md §1A` (e.g. `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_2_2_2 → wan22_vace`, `qwen-image-edit-2509 → qwen_edit`, etc.).
3. **Add** unit tests under `tests/test_route_keys.py`: stability (same inputs → same key), Cohort A/B/E sample inputs match a fixed expected list.

### Step 5: Create golden corpus directory + manifest seeds (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/golden/<route_key>/manifest.json`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Generate** one directory per route_key for:
   - **Cohort A (5):** `qwen_image`, `qwen_image_2512`, `z_image_turbo`, `z_image_turbo_i2i`, `wan_2_2_t2i`.
   - **Cohort B (4):** `qwen_image_edit`, `qwen_image_style`, `image_inpaint`, `annotated_image_edit`.
   - **Representative Cohort E (4):** `individual_travel_segment` (Wan 2.2 i2v baseline), `travel_orchestrator` (single-segment Wan), `travel_stitch`, `join_clips_segment` (Wan 2.2). Defer LTX-control rows to Sprint 9 per §1A row 9-13.
2. **Each `manifest.json`** contains: `route_key`, `task_type`, `model_name`, `guidance_kind`, `continuity_case`, `profile`, `source_fixture` (pointer to `worker_matrix_cases.json` case_id when one exists), `seed`, `expected_artifact_paths`, `calibration_status` matching the YAML.
3. **Add** smoke test `tests/test_golden_corpus.py` asserting each route_key listed in `migration-thresholds.yaml:routes` has a matching manifest and vice versa (no orphan keys).

## Phase 3: Non-RayWorker Product-Contract Fixtures

### Step 6: Author lightweight fixtures for non-RayWorker routes (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/fixtures/non_rayworker/*.json`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** one fixture per route, each capturing payload shape, completion handler path, billing path, expected output shape, owner, and `preserve_or_move` decision sourced from `reigh-worker/docs/migration-baselines.md` §2:
   - `video_enhance.json` — fal.ai handler `fal.py:364`.
   - `image-upscale.json` — fal.ai handler `fal.py:20` (note hyphen/underscore mismatch from §2.1).
   - `animate_character.json` — Wavespeed handler `wavespeed.py:356`.
   - `flux_klein_edit.json` — fal.ai handler `fal.py:276`.
2. **Add** smoke test `tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py` validating each file parses, names a present route, and explicitly records `owner` (interim Peter [REDACTED] per Sprint 0A retro) plus `preserve_or_move`.

## Phase 4: WGP Self-Repeatability Harness and Calibration Run

### Step 7: Add `wgp_self_repeat.py` driver (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/wgp_self_repeat.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** a thin CLI that wraps `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py update --backend wgp --semantic-validation off` to run the same matrix case twice with the same seed, then computes per-metric drift (image pHash, SSIM, dimensions, video frame count, per-frame pHash mean/p95, duration, FPS, latency p95, VRAM peak) and writes:
   - `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/reports/wgp_self_drift_<timestamp>.json`
   - `…/wgp_self_drift_<timestamp>.md`
2. **Compare** drift to thresholds via `Thresholds.for_route(route_key)`; per-route status is `green | exceeds_threshold` and the report records calibration recommendations (widen threshold, mark wgp_only, defer).

### Step 8: Run Cohort A self-drift on RunPod, defer the rest with explicit rationale
**Scope:** Small (live)
1. **Execute** `wgp_self_repeat.py` for Cohort A (image-only, fits the 50 GB pod disk) on a RunPod 4090 pod via the existing `scripts/live_test` harness; downloads the report locally.
2. **Update** `migration-thresholds.yaml`:
   - Move green Cohort A routes from `pending_calibration` → `green`.
   - For any Cohort A route exceeding §11 starting thresholds, widen with explicit calibration note in the YAML and reference the report path.
3. **Mark** all Cohort B and Cohort E rows `deferred_pending_sprint_0c_disk` until the 200 GB bump lands; record the rationale in YAML notes and in the performance review (per memory note that WAN 14B fills 50 GB).

## Phase 5: Documentation Sync and Performance Review

### Step 9: Update live-validation doc (`docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a new section "Threshold and Route Version 0B (2026-05-05)" near the existing 2026-05-05 pilot entries that:
   - Names the YAML path and version string.
   - Links the route-key schema (Step 4) and the golden manifest layout (Step 5).
   - Flags Cohort B / Cohort E live drift as deferred to Sprint 0C disk bump.
2. **Update** the §11 reference and any Sprint 0/3/3.5/11 cross-refs in `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md` to cite the YAML version string.

### Step 10: Add Sprint 0B performance review stub (`docs/megaplan-vibecomfy-chain/performance/sprint-00b-thresholds-golden-corpus.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Author** the review record matching the Sprint 0A template (sprint identity, intended scope, actual outcome, verification, issues, completion assessment, next action) so the chain runner has a place to record results before advancing to Sprint 0C.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 first — the YAML and parser are imported by every later phase's tests.
2. Phase 2 — route-key utility before manifests, manifests before fixture wiring.
3. Phase 3 — non-RayWorker fixtures (independent of GPU) can run in parallel with Phase 2 once route-key utility exists.
4. Phase 4 Step 7 (offline driver) can land before Step 8 (live RunPod run).
5. Phase 5 last; depends on the YAML version string finalized in Phase 4.

## Validation Order
1. Run targeted unit tests in `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/` after each phase's edits.
2. Run `python -m scripts.dual_run_compare.check_thresholds --strict` after Phase 1 and again after each YAML edit in Phase 4.
3. Run `wgp_self_repeat.py` on RunPod for Cohort A only (Phase 4 Step 8); do not attempt Cohort E live until Sprint 0C lands.
4. Final verification: `pytest reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/` plus a fresh `check_thresholds.py` invocation, confirming version, route-key list, and calibration status table.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
# Sprint 0B: Thresholds and Golden Corpus

## Overall Context

This follows Sprint 0A's contract freeze and creates the comparison oracles consumed by every later parity, harness, and canary sprint. The epic cannot rely on subjective media review; later sprints need route-keyed baselines and a single threshold source of truth.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Preserve queue contracts, output shapes, memory-profile behavior, latency expectations, and rollback.
- Use route keys, not friendly names, for baselines and cutover evidence.
- Keep active non-RayWorker routes covered as preservation fixtures even when they stay outside RayWorker.
- Keep `docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md` synchronized with threshold and route versions.

## Sprint Goal

Produce executable comparison oracles and WGP golden corpora for later migration sprints.

## Required Deliverables

- `migration-thresholds.yaml`.
- WGP self-repeatability report.
- Route-keyed WGP golden corpus for Cohort A/B and representative Cohort E routes.
- Lightweight product-contract fixtures for `video_enhance`, `image-upscale`, `animate_character`, and `flux_klein_edit`.
- Live-validation document updated to the same route/threshold version.

## Exit Criteria

Threshold YAML is committed and read by a smoke script; WGP self-drift is below thresholds or affected routes are marked WGP-only/pending; corpus is route-keyed; non-RayWorker active routes have owner-approved fixtures or explicit deferral rationale.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 0B — Thresholds and Golden Corpus

## Overview
Sprint 0A froze the generation contract; Sprint 0B turns the written comparison contract in `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md` §11 into executable artifacts that later dual-run, dry-run, and canary sprints consume. The gate feedback does not indicate the plan targets the wrong root cause: the sprint still needs route-keyed oracles, threshold YAML, WGP repeatability evidence, and preservation fixtures. It does show that several planned mechanics were wrong or underspecified: the WGP driver must use the real `scripts/live_test/main.py --variant fresh|update` interface, threshold defaults must preserve every §11 metric row, route keys need explicit direct-route aliases plus Cohort B edit dimensions, and non-RayWorker fixture validation must check the active orchestrator registry.

Key constraints:
- `reigh-worker/` is a nested git repo. Artifacts under `reigh-worker/` are committed there; top-level docs are committed in the workspace repo.
- §11 fixes the artifact home: `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/migration-thresholds.yaml` and `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/golden/<route_key>/`.
- Cohort A/B manifests use canonical direct route keys when §11 names a product route directly, such as `qwen_image` or `qwen_image_edit`. Expanded derived keys are used for dimensional variants where needed, especially Cohort B edit-case variants and Cohort E guidance/continuity/profile cases.
- Live WGP calibration may be blocked by RunPod disk capacity. Sprint 0B must still commit a report artifact: either a live self-drift report for calibrated routes or a machine-readable pending/deferred report that marks each uncalibrated route `pending_calibration`, `deferred_pending_sprint_0c_disk`, or `wgp_only` with rationale.

## Phase 1: Threshold Artifact and Smoke Reader

### Step 1: Audit §11 threshold rows and current consumers (`docs/migration-vibecomfy.md`, `reigh-worker/scripts/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirm** the §11 table in `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md` is still the source of truth.
2. **Extract** each concrete threshold row at row granularity into a fixed metric key list, preserving distinct entries for image pHash, image SSIM, pixel dimensions, output format, video frame count, video duration, video FPS, video per-frame pHash mean, video per-frame pHash p95, audio duration, latency p95, VRAM peak, OOM allowance, and canary divergence.
3. **Confirm** no existing `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/` tree would be overwritten.

### Step 2: Author `migration-thresholds.yaml` (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/migration-thresholds.yaml`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the YAML with these top-level fields:
   - `version: "0B-2026-05-05"`.
   - `schema_version: 1`.
   - `metric_keys:` listing every §11 row key explicitly.
   - `defaults:` keyed by the same metric keys, with each entry carrying `metric`, `threshold`, `failure_action`, and optional `applies_to`.
   - `routes:` keyed by canonical `route_key`, with `cohort`, `calibration_status`, `notes`, and optional per-route `overrides` keyed by metric key.
2. **Use one calibration status enum everywhere:** `green | pending_calibration | deferred_pending_sprint_0c_disk | wgp_only | owner_deferred`.
3. **Seed** Cohort A/B and representative Cohort E route entries. Cohort B/E entries blocked by the current 50 GB pod disk use `deferred_pending_sprint_0c_disk`; routes awaiting live work but not disk-blocked use `pending_calibration`.

### Step 3: Add threshold parser and smoke CLI (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/__init__.py`, `thresholds.py`, `check_thresholds.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `thresholds.py:load_thresholds(path) -> Thresholds` as typed dataclasses with `version`, `metric_keys`, `defaults`, `routes`, and `for_route(route_key)` that merges default metric entries with route overrides without collapsing metric identity.
2. **Implement** `check_thresholds.py` as `python -m scripts.dual_run_compare.check_thresholds [--strict]`, run from `reigh-worker`, validating schema version, metric row completeness, route status enum membership, route override keys, and manifest sync when manifests exist.
3. **Add** tests in `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_thresholds.py` covering load, required metric rows, override merge, invalid status rejection, and missing metric rejection.

## Phase 2: Route-Key Schema and Golden Corpus Layout

### Step 4: Define [REDACTED] (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/route_keys.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `canonical_route_key(...)` with explicit modes:
   - Direct route mode for Cohort A/B product routes returns the stable product route key, e.g. `qwen_image`, `qwen_image_2512`, `z_image_turbo`, `z_image_turbo_i2i`, `wan_2_2_t2i`, `qwen_image_edit`, `qwen_image_style`, `image_inpaint`, `annotated_image_edit`.
   - Cohort B variant mode includes edit-model variant, mask case, annotation case, style-reference case, and profile when those dimensions are present, using a documented slug format such as `<task_type>__<edit_model_family>__mask-<case>__annotation-<case>__style_ref-<case>__profile<N>`.
   - Cohort E mode uses `<task_type>__<model_family>__<guidance_kind>__<continuity_case>__profile<N>`.
2. **Implement** model-family mapping for the models referenced by `worker_matrix_cases.json` and §1A, including Wan 2.2, Qwen edit/image variants, Z-Image, and LTX entries that may be deferred.
3. **Document** alias behavior in the module docstring so YAML keys, manifest directories, and downstream callers use the same canonical route key.
4. **Add** `tests/test_route_keys.py` for direct Cohort A/B aliases, Cohort B dimensional variants, Cohort E dimensional keys, slug normalization, and deterministic output.

### Step 5: Create golden corpus manifests (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/golden/<route_key>/manifest.json`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** one manifest directory per required route key:
   - Cohort A direct routes: `qwen_image`, `qwen_image_2512`, `z_image_turbo`, `z_image_turbo_i2i`, `wan_2_2_t2i`.
   - Cohort B direct preservation routes: `qwen_image_edit`, `qwen_image_style`, `image_inpaint`, `annotated_image_edit`.
   - Representative Cohort E routes: `individual_travel_segment`, `travel_orchestrator`, `travel_stitch`, `join_clips_segment`, each with Wan 2.2 model/profile metadata; LTX-control rows remain deferred unless §11 requires them now.
2. **For every manifest**, include `route_key`, `cohort`, `task_type`, `model_name`, `model_family`, `guidance_kind`, `continuity_case`, Cohort B edit dimensions when applicable, `profile`, `source_fixture`, `source_fixture_status`, `seed`, `expected_artifact_paths`, `calibration_status`, and `notes`.
3. **Handle missing worker matrix coverage explicitly:** manifests whose cases do not exist in `worker_matrix_cases.json` use `source_fixture_status: seed_fixture_required` and include a minimal local seed payload reference under `fixtures/golden_seed_payloads/` rather than pretending the worker matrix contains them.
4. **Add** `tests/test_golden_corpus.py` asserting every YAML route has a manifest, every manifest has a YAML route, direct route keys match the canonicalizer, and missing matrix cases are explicitly marked.

## Phase 3: Non-RayWorker Product-Contract Fixtures

### Step 6: Author preservation fixtures (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/fixtures/non_rayworker/*.json`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** one fixture per active non-RayWorker route: `video_enhance`, `image-upscale`, `animate_character`, and `flux_klein_edit`.
2. **Each fixture** records payload shape, completion handler reference, billing path, expected output shape, interim owner, `preserve_or_move`, route-key spelling, and any known alias mismatch such as `image-upscale` versus `image_upscale`.
3. **Source** handler references from `reigh-worker/docs/migration-baselines.md` and validate active route spellings against `reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/task_handlers.py`.
4. **Add** `tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py` validating JSON parse, required fields, named owner, preserve/move decision, and presence in the active handler registry, including the hyphenated `image-upscale` key.

## Phase 4: WGP Self-Repeatability Harness and Report

### Step 7: Add `wgp_self_repeat.py` using the real live-test interface (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/wgp_self_repeat.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the driver around the actual CLI shape: `python scripts/live_test/main.py --variant fresh|update --wgp-profile <profile> --timeout <seconds> ...`, matching the parser in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py` and dispatch through `variant_fresh.run(args)` / `variant_update.run(args)`.
2. **Do not** pass nonexistent `update` positional args, `--backend`, or `--semantic-validation` flags. Semantic/media comparison is owned by `wgp_self_repeat.py` after artifacts are collected.
3. **Run** the same route/matrix case twice with the same seed where live execution is available; otherwise support `--record-deferral` to emit a pending/deferred report without GPU access.
4. **Compute** row-granular drift metrics where artifacts exist: image pHash, SSIM, dimensions, output format, video frame count, duration, FPS, per-frame pHash mean/p95, audio duration, latency p95, VRAM peak, OOM state, and canary divergence placeholders where applicable.
5. **Write** paired JSON and Markdown reports under `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/reports/`, and compare each metric against `Thresholds.for_route(route_key)`.

### Step 8: Produce Sprint 0B repeatability evidence (`reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/reports/`)
**Scope:** Small to Medium
1. **Attempt** Cohort A live WGP-vs-WGP repeatability on RunPod using the existing live-test harness and the real `--variant` / `--wgp-profile` arguments.
2. **If live execution succeeds**, commit the downloaded Cohort A report and update YAML route statuses to `green` or `wgp_only` / `pending_calibration` with report references.
3. **If live execution is blocked by disk, credentials, pod availability, or hardware access**, run `wgp_self_repeat.py --record-deferral` to create a committed Sprint 0B report artifact listing every required route, its attempted command shape, blocker, status, and next calibration action. This keeps the deliverable executable and machine-readable while preserving the sprint's requirement that uncalibrated routes are explicitly pending/deferred.
4. **Mark** Cohort B/E rows that cannot run on the current 50 GB pod as `deferred_pending_sprint_0c_disk` with notes referencing Sprint 0C's 200 GB disk prerequisite.

## Phase 5: Documentation Sync and Performance Review

### Step 9: Update live-validation and migration docs (`docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md`, `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `Threshold and Route Version 0B (2026-05-05)` to `docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md`, naming the YAML path, version string, metric row list, [REDACTED] module, golden manifest layout, report path, and calibration status summary.
2. **Update** §11 and Sprint 0/3/3.5/11 cross-references in `docs/migration-vibecomfy.md` to cite the YAML version and explain that the YAML is the executable threshold source of truth.

### Step 10: Add Sprint 0B performance review stub (`docs/megaplan-vibecomfy-chain/performance/sprint-00b-thresholds-golden-corpus.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Author** the review record matching the Sprint 0A template: sprint identity, intended scope, actual outcome, verification, known blockers, calibration status, completion assessment, and next action.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 first: row-granular thresholds and parser are the contract used by later tests.
2. Phase 2 next: [REDACTED] must exist before manifests are seeded.
3. Phase 3 can run after route-key constants are stable; its registry validation reaches into `reigh-worker-orchestrator` but does not change orchestrator code.
4. Phase 4 driver lands before live or deferral reports. The report artifact is required even if GPU execution is unavailable.
5. Phase 5 last, after the YAML version, route list, and report status are final.

## Validation Order
1. Run `python -m scripts.dual_run_compare.check_thresholds --strict` from `reigh-worker` after Phase 1 and again after each YAML or manifest change.
2. Run focused tests in `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/` after each phase.
3. Run `pytest reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/` for final local validation without GPU access.
4. Validate the WGP report path exists and is referenced by YAML and live-validation docs. Live RunPod success is recorded when available; otherwise the committed deferral report is the Sprint 0B evidence artifact.
5. Confirm non-RayWorker fixture tests read `reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/task_handlers.py` and preserve the exact `image-upscale` route spelling.

    "idea": "# Sprint 0B: Thresholds and Golden Corpus\n\n## Overall Context\n\nThis follows Sprint 0A's contract freeze and creates the comparison oracles consumed by every later parity, harness, and canary sprint. The epic cannot rely on subjective media review; later sprints need route-keyed baselines and a single threshold source of truth.\n\n## Shared Operating Rules\n\n- Preserve queue contracts, output shapes, memory-profile behavior, latency expectations, and rollback.\n- Use route keys, not friendly names, for baselines and cutover evidence.\n- Keep active non-RayWorker routes covered as preservation fixtures even when they stay outside RayWorker.\n- Keep `docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md` synchronized with threshold and route versions.\n\n## Sprint Goal\n\nProduce executable comparison oracles and WGP golden corpora for later migration sprints.\n\n## Required Deliverables\n\n- `migration-thresholds.yaml`.\n- WGP self-repeatability report.\n- Route-keyed WGP golden corpus for Cohort A/B and representative Cohort E routes.\n- Lightweight product-contract fixtures for `video_enhance`, `image-upscale`, `animate_character`, and `flux_klein_edit`.\n- Live-validation document updated to the same route/threshold version.\n\n## Exit Criteria\n\nThreshold YAML is committed and read by a smoke script; WGP self-drift is below thresholds or affected routes are marked WGP-only/pending; corpus is route-keyed; non-RayWorker active routes have owner-approved fixtures or explicit deferral rationale.",

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
